 Baptist Ghurch, Birmingham, Alabama,

1 - At 10:19 a.m., on September 15, 1963, there was an
 explosion, generally believed to have been caused by ten or
§?3 more sticks of dynamite, at the 16th Street Baptist Church,
 Birmingham, Alabama Negro!, taking the lives of four
 children, who were among approximately 100-125 people in
 attendance at Sunday School. Twenty or more persons were
as; J.
 in ured. The church is located at the corner of 6th Avenue
 and 16th Street, North, near the downtown section of the city.
 =_.&#39;~. The point of impact was outside the church, a brick building,
 on the 16th Street side under some concrete steps and against
 _. .... the wall. Damage to the church building was estimated to be
 Anhiii-Pin: :-
 $30,000-$40,000 and undetermined amount of damage to surround
 -"-E-E.
:5 ,5,-i
 . ing buildings.
 ~._-11*
 This church
 had been a meeting place in the past
 t for racial demonstrators. This bombing, one of aseries in .
 &#39;. the recent past in Birmingham, is possibly attributable to
 racial extremists. _

 &#39;
 The crime scene search has failed to develop
 evidence indicating that the explosion was detonated by any
 specific type of detonator. it is unknown at this point
 whether a fuse or a timing mechanism was used.

 greater intensity, possibly several sticks
 of dynamite,
 occurred at the corner adjacent to the captioned address.
 This second explosion shattered
 a telephone pole, blew
 a a
 -I _;: small crater in the curbing and showered the neighborhood
-er:-1-

 which caused extensive shrapnel damage to surrounding property

,,_..
 1:-.
 fragments. First explosion believed to have occurred within
 *:1.:92 ten

 <rs~ neighbors and police to be injured

 . -&#39;--5 told, that such close cooperation prejudices the FBI and results in no

 That cooperation

 local authorities. I personally do not approve oi this policy because I think

.».~-I-:- -1- iavored them ii they violated any Federal statute. As a matter oi tact, our

 .;.
L F5? Another aspect outthis matter which I think should be drawn ,

_.,-38::
 I =&#39;-i-.

 Martin Luther King several months ago made a speech in which he claimed

 , 1 E; j.&#39;_.;

 _§?,;.;§ report matters to that office. We have iive Agents at Albany, Georgia, and

 in what could be called the
 &#39;
 deep South. I made an effort to effect an appointment with Martin Luther

---m I but he refused to make an appointment so I, therefore, have to conclude

 of his statement in the South and the statements oi
 other Negro leaders has resulted in the impression that practically all of
 our southern offices are manned by Agents who were born in the South.
